TI On the Usefulness of Promises
SO PROCEEDINGS OF THE 2026 ANNUAL ACM-SIAM SYMPOSIUM ON DISCRETE
   ALGORITHMS, SODA
SE Applied and Computational Discrete Algorithms
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 37th Symposium on Discrete Algorithms-SODA-Annual
CY JAN 11-14, 2026
CL Vancouver, CANADA
SP Society for Industrial and Applied Mathematics, ACM SIGACT
ID CONSTRAINT SATISFACTION; ALGEBRAIC STRUCTURE
AB A Boolean predicate A is defined to be promise-useful if PCSP(A, B) is tractable for some non-trivial Boolean predicate B and otherwise it is promise-useless. We initiate investigations of this notion and derive sufficient conditions for both promise-usefulness and promise-uselessness (assuming P not equal NP). While we do not obtain a complete characterization, our conditions are sufficient to classify all predicates of arity at most 4 and almost all predicates of arity 5. We also derive asymptotic results to show that for large arities a vast majority of all predicates are promise-useless.
   Our results are primarily obtained by a thorough study of the "Promise-SAT" problem, in which we are given a k-SAT instance with the promise that there is a satisfying assignment for which the literal values of each clause satisfy some additional constraint.
   The algorithmic results are based on the basic LP + affine IP algorithm of Brakensiek et al. (SICOMP, 2020) while we use a number of novel criteria to establish NP-hardness.
C1 [Austrin, Per; Hastad, Johan; Martinsson, Bjorn] KTH Royal Inst Technol, Stockholm, Sweden.

PT J
AU Hansson, SO
AF Hansson, Sven Ove
TI Delineating the Legitimate Role of Values in Science
SO JOURNAL FOR GENERAL PHILOSOPHY OF SCIENCE
LA English
DT Article; Early Access
DE All-purpose beliefs; Cognitive limitations; Context-sensitivity of
   beliefs; Credences; Fact-finding practices; Fact-value distinction; Full
   beliefs; Scientific corpus; Uncertain beliefs; Values in science
ID PRAGMATIC ENCROACHMENT; PARTIAL BELIEF; ACCEPTANCE; RISK; EVIDENTIALISM;
   PROBABILITY
AB Previous philosophical discussions of values in science have mainly been devoted to showing that non-epistemic values can have a legitimate influence on the evidential requirements for accepting a scientific claim. There is a lack of credible explanations of how the influence of such values can be - and usually is - adequately restricted so that science still provides us with sufficiently reliable information. The importance of this problem has increased due to the current onslaught of science denial. We show how it can be solved with decision-theoretical tools, the most important of which is the distinction between lowering and raising evidential requirements. Lowering the evidential requirements for treating a claim as scientifically established is usually inadvisable since it increases the risk of adopting badly supported - and therefore often incorrect - full beliefs. In such cases, a distinction should instead be drawn between the criteria for accepting a claim and those for acting as if it is true. In contrast, value-based raising of the evidential requirements for accepting a claim does not involve any risk of making the set of factual full beliefs less reliable. This type of value influence seems to have a considerable role in areas of science that have extensive practical applications. The influence of values in science may therefore be larger than what has often been assumed.
C1 [Hansson, Sven Ove] Royal Inst Technol KTH, Div Philosophy, Stockholm, Sweden.

TI Room Impulse Response Generation Conditioned on Acoustic Parameters
SO 2025 IEEE WORKSHOP ON APPLICATIONS OF SIGNAL PROCESSING TO AUDIO AND
   ACOUSTICS, WASPAA
SE IEEE Workshop on Applications of Signal Processing to Audio and
   Acoustics
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 Workshop on Applications of Signal Processing to Audio and
   Acoustics-WASPAA-Annual
CY OCT 12-15, 2025
CL Tahoe Vista, CA
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc, Amazon.com Inc, CISCO USA, Mitsubishi Electric Research Laboratories Inc, Meta AI, Treble Capital LLC, Apple Inc, ByteDance Inc, Google LLC, Microsoft Corp, Yamaha, Bose Corp, University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign
AB The generation of room impulse responses (RIRs) using deep neural networks has attracted growing research interest due to its applications in virtual and augmented reality, audio postproduction, and related fields. Most existing approaches condition generative models on physical descriptions of a room, such as its size, shape, and surface materials. However, this reliance on geometric information limits their usability in scenarios where the room layout is unknown or when perceptual realism (how a space sounds to a listener) is more important than strict physical accuracy. In this study, we propose an alternative strategy: conditioning RIR generation directly on a set of RIR acoustic parameters. These parameters include various measures of reverberation time and direct sound to reverberation ratio, both broadband and bandwise. By specifying how the space should sound instead of how it should look, our method enables more flexible and perceptually driven RIR generation. We explore both autoregressive and non-autoregressive generative models operating in the Descript Audio Codec domain, using either discrete token sequences or continuous embeddings. Specifically, we have selected four models to evaluate: an autoregressive transformer, the MaskGIT model, a flow matching model, and a classifier-based approach. Objective and subjective evaluations are performed to compare these methods with state-of-the-art alternatives. Results show that the proposed models match or outperform state-of-the-art alternatives, with the MaskGIT model achieving the best performance.

AU Taghavian, H

AF Taghavian, Hamed
   Andersson, Malin

TI Model-free fast charging of lithium-ion batteries by online gradient
   descent
SO AUTOMATICA
LA English
DT Article
DE Battery management systems; Data-driven controllers; Online convex
   optimization
AB This paper studies the fast-charging problem of lithium-ion batteries with multiple safety and aging constraints. It is noted that, under suitable conditions, the optimal solutions to these problems are of the bang-ride type; they follow the constraint-set boundary to maximize the charging current. We exploit this observation to propose a data-driven algorithm based on online gradient descent, which converges to the bang-ride protocol by observing the measurable battery quantities, such as input current, terminal voltage, and temperature. As such, the proposed method can optimize the charging current without requiring detailed battery models or training episodes. The theoretical convergence is proven under mild conditions and is numerically validated on several linear and nonlinear battery models, including single-particle and equivalent-circuit models. (c) 2026 The Author(s). Published by Elsevier Ltd. This is an open access article under the CC BY license (http://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/4.0/).

TI Dissecting CPU-GPU Unified Physical Memory on AMD MI300A APUs
SO 2025 IEEE INTERNATIONAL SYMPOSIUM ON WORKLOAD CHARACTERIZATION, IISWC
SE International Symposium on Workload Characterization Proceedings
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 28th International Symposium on Workload Characterization-IISWC-Annual
CY OCT 12-14, 2025
CL Irvine, CA
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc, Intel Corporation, Futurewei Technologies Inc, Panmnesia
DE Memory System Characterization; GPU Memory Management; High Performance
   Computing (HPC)
AB Discrete GPUs are a cornerstone of HPC and data center systems, requiring management of separate CPU and GPU memory spaces. Unified Virtual Memory (UVM) has been proposed to ease the burden of memory management; however, at a high cost in performance. The recent introduction of AMD's MI300A Accelerated Processing Units (APUs)-as deployed in the El Capitan supercomputer-enables HPC systems featuring integrated CPU and GPU with Unified Physical Memory (UPM) for the first time. This work presents the first comprehensive characterization of the UPM architecture on MI300A. We first analyze the UPM system properties, including memory latency, bandwidth, and coherence overhead. We then assess the efficiency of the system software in memory allocation, page fault handling, TLB management, and Infinity Cache utilization. We propose a set of porting strategies for transforming applications for the UPM architecture and evaluate six applications on the MI300A APU. Our results show that applications on UPM using the unified memory model can match or outperform those in the explicitly managed model-while reducing memory costs by up to 44%.

AU Troubitsyna, E
AF Troubitsyna, Elena
GP IEEE COMPUTER SOC
TI Modelling Collaborative Resilient Behavior of Multi-Agent Systems
SO 2025 IEEE INTERNATIONAL CONFERENCE ON AUTONOMIC COMPUTING AND
   SELF-ORGANIZING SYSTEMS COMPANION, ACSOS-C
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 6th International Conference on Autonomic Computing and Self-Organizing
   Systems-ACSOS-Annual
CY SEP 29-OCT 03, 2025
CL Tokyo, JAPAN
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc, IEEE Computer Society, Google LLC
DE multi-agent systems; mission planning; fault tolerance; coordination
AB Effective collaboration among agents is essential to allow multi-agent systems to accomplish their objectives. When designing such systems for mission-critical scenarios like surveillance or disaster response, it is necessary to ensure that operations can be completed successfully even in the face of agent malfunctions or unforeseen environmental changes. Consequently, resilience becomes a key design priority. However, achieving it is challenging due to the inherently decentralized control and asynchronous communication typical of these systems. In this work, we present a formal framework that models cooperative behaviors in multi-agent systems with the aim of supporting resilience. The framework addresses issues of dynamic reconfiguration and adaptive task redistribution. Furthermore, we introduce specialized mechanisms for inter-agent knowledge exchange and coordination, which strengthen the system's ability to withstand agent failures.

AF Bruijl, Ivo M.

TI Review on the modelling of modular POD-based passenger and goods
   transportation
SO EUROPEAN TRANSPORT RESEARCH REVIEW
LA English
DT Review
DE Modular road transport; Modular rail transport; Modular logistics;
   Multimodality; Co-modality; Demand-responsive transit
ID ALGORITHM
AB Autonomous modular vehicles create new possibilities within public transportation services, responding to growing needs for flexible and sustainable transport. Modularity allows for vehicle configurations with standardised load units, enabling multimodal (i.e., interoperable across multiple modes, such as road and rail) and co-modal (i.e., integration of both passenger and goods) transportation systems. Although Demand Responsive Transit (DRT) systems see a substantial body of literature, this is the first comprehensive review focusing on modularity, multimodality, and co-modality in public transportation. This review synthesises 45 peer-reviewed articles, identified through a systematic Scopus search and enriched by snowball sampling, across three dimensions: transport system design, modularity implementation, and optimisation methodologies. The results show a concentration of research on road-based systems, whereas modular systems across multiple transportation modes remain largely underexplored. Similarly, most research attention is given to line- or corridor-based systems, highlighting the need to further explore systems with a more flexible network design to leverage the benefits that modular vehicles offer. Co-modality receives growing interest, but has not yet been systematically analysed from a modular perspective. Methodologically, most studies rely on analytical methods to optimise planning and operations, with less attention to simulation-based methods, which may better include real-world uncertainties, and Artificial Intelligence-based (AI-based) methods, which offer promising opportunities for more robust, flexible and scalable modular transport systems. This review contributes to the field of POD-based multimodal transportation as the first structured overview of multimodal and co-modal aspects in modular transport systems, identifying key research gaps and outlining directions for future research.

AU Daei, S

AF Daei, Sajad

GP IEEE
TI When Near Becomes Far: From Rayleigh to Optimal Near-Field and Far-Field
   Boundaries
SO GLOBECOM 2025-2025 IEEE GLOBAL COMMUNICATIONS CONFERENCE
SE IEEE Global Telecommunications Conference (Globecom)
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 Global Communications Conference-GLOBECOM
CY DEC 08-12, 2025
CL Taipei, TAIWAN
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc
DE Near-field propagation; Rayleigh distance; millimeter-wave and sub-THz
   arrays; massive MIMO
ID MASSIVE MIMO
AB The transition toward 6G is pushing wireless communication into a regime where the classical plane-wave assumption no longer holds. Millimeter-wave and sub-THz frequencies shrink wavelengths to millimeters, while meter-scale arrays featuring hundreds of antenna elements dramatically enlarge the aperture. Together, these trends collapse the classical Rayleigh far-field boundary from kilometers to mere singledigit meters. Consequently, most practical 6G indoor, vehicular, and industrial deployments will inherently operate within the radiating near-field, where reliance on the plane-wave approximation leads to severe array-gain losses, degraded localization accuracy, and excessive pilot overhead. This paper re-examines the fundamental question: "Where does the far-field truly begin?" Rather than adopting purely geometric definitions, we introduce an application-oriented approach based on user-defined error budgets and a rigorous Fresnel-zone analysis that fully accounts for both amplitude and phase curvature. We propose three practical mismatch metrics: worst-case element mismatch, worst-case normalized mean square error, and spectral efficiency loss. For each metric, we derive a provably optimal transition distancethe minimal range beyond which mismatch permanently remains below a given tolerance-and provide closed-form solutions. Extensive numerical evaluations across diverse frequencies and antenna-array dimensions show that our proposed thresholds can exceed the Rayleigh distance by more than an order of magnitude. By transforming the near-field from a design nuisance into a precise, quantifiable tool, our results provide a clear roadmap for enabling reliable and resource-efficient nearfield communications and sensing in emerging 6G systems.
C1 [Daei, Sajad; Fodor, Gabor; Skoglund, Mikael] KTH Royal Inst Technol, Sch Elect Engn Comp Sci, Stockholm, Sweden.

AU Prasetia, ACG
AF Prasetia, Ari Carisza Graha
TI Agents of collaborative adaptation: modelling temporal resilience in
   supply chains
SO ANNALS OF OPERATIONS RESEARCH
LA English
DT Article; Early Access
DE Agent-based modelling; Adaptive agents; Policy learning; Supply chain
   resilience; Cascading failures; Decentralized decision-making
ID NETWORK; DISRUPTION; TRUST
AB Supply-chain disruptions vary in intensity, scale, and frequency, producing temporally heterogeneous impacts on network viability. The emphasis on exposure and topology has anchored resilience in network structure, obscuring how collaboration and trust govern its phase-specific temporal dynamics, including resistive capabilities, adaptation speed, containment, and recovery. This study thus develops an agent-based simulation of the Netherlands logistics network, in which nodes operate as autonomous agents that redistribute load under capacity, link, and trust constraints. Cascading failures are simulated with trust-gated, capacity-aware, and collaboration-dependent load reallocation, capturing both immediate contagion and multi-step redistribution dynamics. The framework compares rule-based heuristics with adaptive AI agents trained using supervised imitation, online multiplicative adaptation, and cross-entropy policy search. Temporal resilience is then measured across each phase specific metrics of absorptive, adaptive, and recovery phases across the collaboration structure. This points to the main contribution of this article, in which AI agents improve adaptation and cascade containment through targeted use of high-trust, high-capacity links, while recovery dynamics remain bounded by structural and exogenous constraints. This points to a layered view of resilience, where performance depends on the interaction between network configuration, trust-mediated coordination, and agent-level adaptation.

TI Refined PAC-Bayes Bounds for Offline Bandits
SO 2025 IEEE INTERNATIONAL SYMPOSIUM ON INFORMATION THEORY, ISIT
SE IEEE International Symposium on Information Theory
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 International Symposium on Information Theory-ISIT-Annual
CY JUN 22-27, 2025
CL Ann Arbor, MI
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc, IEEE Information Theory Society
AB In this paper, we present refined probabilistic bounds on empirical reward estimates for off-policy learning in bandit problems. We build on the PAC-Bayesian bounds from [1]-[4] and improve on their results using a new parameter optimization approach introduced by [5]. This technique is based on a discretization of the space of possible events to optimize the "in probability" parameter, lambda. We provide two parameter-free PAC-Bayes bounds, one based on Hoeffding-Azuma's inequality and the other based on Bernstein's inequality. We prove that our bounds are almost optimal as they recover the same rate as would be obtained by setting the "in probability" parameter after the realization of the data.

TI Information-Theoretic Minimax Regret Upper Bounds for Reinforcement
   Learning Problems
SO 2025 IEEE INFORMATION THEORY WORKSHOP, ITW
SE Information Theory Workshop
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 Information Theory Workshop-ITW
CY SEP 29-OCT 03, 2025
CL Sydney, AUSTRALIA
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc, University of Sydney
DE reinforcement learning; information theory; minimax theorems
AB We study different classes of reinforcement learning problems using the minimax regret framework. We formalize a finite-horizon reinforcement learning problem setting that is suitable for the information-theoretic analysis of minimax regret which encompasses linear bandits, Markov decision processes, linear Markov decision processes, and other reinforcement learning problems. We derive a minimax theorem applicable to this setting that does not require any finiteness or deterministic policy constraints. Using this theorem, we show that any Bayesian regret bound can be used to bound the minimax regret within our framework. We then apply the minimax theorem to obtain an information-theoretic upper bound for the minimax regret, leveraging a general Bayesian regret bound. The derived minimax regret bound inherits key properties of the Bayesian regret bound, including its ability to isolate factors such as the information ratio, the mutual information between the learning target and the environment, and the Bayesian regret of the target policy. Finally, we demonstrate the applicability of our bounds in various settings, including linear bandits, episodic reinforcement learning, and linear Markov decision processes, recovering known results for the minimax regret.

TI Classifying Dynamics with Many Symmetries
SO PROCEEDINGS OF THE INTERNATIONAL CONGRESS OF MATHEMATICIANS 2026, VOL 5
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2026 International Congress of Mathematicians-ICM-Quadrennial
CY JUL 23-30, 2026
CL Philadelphia, PA
DE group actions; partial hyperbolicity; rigidity
ID PARTIALLY HYPERBOLIC ACTIONS; LOCAL RIGIDITY; ANOSOV ACTIONS;
   COHOMOLOGICAL EQUATION; GLOBAL RIGIDITY; ABELIAN ACTIONS; ACTIONS I.;
   CLASSIFICATION; COCYCLES; SMOOTH
AB We present an overview of some recent advances in the direction of global, local, and semilocal smooth classification of dynamical systems that embed in large groups of smooth transformations. The groups may be higher-rank semisimple, or higher-rank abelian, or may be groups of symmetries. All the presented rigidity results have similar flavor: a dynamical system embedding in a large group, satisfying certain dynamical features, is necessarily smoothly conjugate to an algebraic model. However, there is a broad variety of methods and approaches, since settings and dynamics may be very different. This text attempts to provide a glimpse of the diverse mechanisms that lead to the smooth rigidity phenomena for groups of transformations, and give the taste of how different techniques, originating in different directions in dynamics, are combined.

TI Using network databases for data fusion for the study of the promotion
   of youth mental well-being
SO HEALTH INFORMATICS JOURNAL
LA English
DT Article
DE data fusion; graph database; mental well-being; youth mental health;
   Neo4j; public health informatics; data integration; Sweden; property
   graph
ID HEALTH-PROMOTION; GLOBAL BURDEN; NEIGHBORHOODS; ADOLESCENTS; DISEASE;
   IMPACT
AB Promoting mental well-being among young people is a pressing need for Sweden to mitigate declines in health among the next generation. To achieve this, various programs have been implemented at both the school and municipal levels. Different municipalities implement programs, monitor, and collect data on youth mental well-being within their areas. Analysis of these data is challenging because data silos exist across institutions. System-level assessment has been difficult in both the reasoning and decision-making phases, and in attributing the impact of these strategies to the specific actions taken by municipalities.In this work, we propose using a data fusion approach to create a common dataset for studying youth mental well-being by combining data from various departments across Swedish municipalities. We identify the required datasets, along with their schemas, metadata, and definitions, and develop a graph database design that defines standard terms and related concepts. We deploy this min two Stockholm municipalities to demonstrate the approach's ability to query across different institutional datasets through the common graph schema. Finally, we outline the use of a graph database in a participatory setting to better understand programs that promote youth mental well-being across departments in both municipalities.

TI Study of Transformer Pressboard Impregnation Process through Sequential
   Dielectric Spectroscopy Measurements
SO 2025 IEEE CONFERENCE ON ELECTRICAL INSULATION AND DIELECTRIC PHENOMENA,
   CEIDP
SE Conference on Electrical Insulation and Dielectric Phenomena Annual
   Report
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 Conference on Electrical Insulation and Dielectric
   Phenomena-CEIDP-Annual
CY SEP 14-17, 2025
CL University of Manchester, Manchester, ENGLAND
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc, IEEE Dielectrics and Electrical Insulation Society, Doble Engineering Co, GE Vernova, National Grid PLC, Hitachi Limited
HO University of Manchester
DE power transformers; mineral oil; impregnation; pressboard; dielectric
   spectroscopy
AB The power transformer is one of the main and most essential components in the electrical power system, which enables efficient voltage conversion for power transmission and distribution. The power transformer performance mainly relies on the insulation system which generally consists of mineral oil, and solid materials like paper, and pressboards. This insulation system has several roles like insulating the electrical components, and ensuring the power transformer safety. A critical process is the impregnation of the solid cellulosic materials with the dielectric liquids to eliminate any air pockets that otherwise would form weak spots and eventually lead to the formation of partial discharges which can damage the insulation system. Optimization of the impregnation process is important for energy efficient manufacturing of power transformers. This paper studies the oil impregnation process of transformer pressboard through sequential dielectric spectroscopy measurements. The pressboard was a 2 mm-thick vacuum-dried high-density pressboard, and the oil a dried standard transformer mineral oil. The impregnation process was analyzed through sequential frequency dielectric spectroscopy (FDS) measurements with a Megger IDAX 300 in the frequency range 10 mHz - 1000 Hz. The impregnation process was studied at three temperatures: 23 degrees C, 40 degrees C, and 60 degrees C through the increase in the system's effective capacitance vs impregnation time in the higher frequency range of the spectra - i.e. 1 Hz - 1000 Hz. At lower frequencies the interpretation is more complicated due to other dielectric mechanisms such as interfacial charge accumulation.

DE discharge; transient; PRPD; monitoring
ID SPACE-CHARGE
AB Partial discharge (PD) characterization under combined AC and repetitive transient stress is essential for assessing insulation integrity in high-voltage oil/paper systems. This study examines PD behavior under 50 Hz AC voltage with superimposed standard lightning impulses (LI) (1.2/50 mu s) at two different repeated interval of 60 s and 180 s. PD activity is analyzed through time-domain evolution, frequency-domain characteristics, phase-resolved patterns (PRPD), and the statistical distribution of time between discharge events. The results show that higher impulse repetition rates intensify PD activity, promote phase clustering, and broaden spectral components indicating greater field stress and reduced charge relaxation. These investigations support the development of effective on-line condition monitoring and aid in diagnosing strategies for high voltage equipment.

TI Symmetric tilt grain boundaries in uranium nitride and uranium carbide
SO SCIENTIFIC REPORTS
LA English
DT Article
ID ENERGIES; FUEL; MONONITRIDE; DEPENDENCE; MAGNESIA; SCHEMES; IMPACT
AB The development of UN as a nuclear fuel requires good understanding of its microstructure. Here is presented a study of a set of 14 symmetric tilt grain boundaries (GBs) in UN and UC using density functional theory (DFT). From this set, both for UN and UC, the Sigma 03 (111)[10] is the most stable GB. Correlations between properties of the GBs such as tilt angle, excess volume and energies were obtained. For both materials the GB energies have a global minimum at the tilt angle of 70 degrees. The plot of the tilt angles vs. GB energies converges naturally to the previously published stacking fault energy for UN at 0 degrees. Using this method, a stacking fault energy for UC was obtained. The correlation between GB excess volume and GB energy is strong for UN, it is single-valued and of the simple parabolic type and shows that the excess volume is a good predictor of the GB energy for this material. For UC, the corresponding correlation is moderately predictive. Similarly to what is known for MgO with rock salt structure, this set of GBs is expected to be a good description of coincidence site lattice GBs in UN and UC that occur in microstructures close to equilibrium.

TI An Information Geometric Approach to Local Information Privacy with
   Applications to Max-lift and Local Differential Privacy
SO 2025 IEEE INFORMATION THEORY WORKSHOP, ITW
SE Information Theory Workshop
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 Information Theory Workshop-ITW
CY SEP 29-OCT 03, 2025
CL Sydney, AUSTRALIA
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc, University of Sydney
ID DESIGN
AB We study an information-theoretic privacy mechanism design, where an agent observes useful data Y and wants to reveal the information to a user. Since the useful data is correlated with the private data X, the agent uses a privacy mechanism to produce disclosed data U that can be released. We assume that the agent observes Y and has no direct access to X, i.e., the private data is hidden. We study the privacy mechanism design that maximizes the revealed information about Y while satisfying a bounded Local Information Privacy (LIP) criterion. When the leakage is sufficiently small, concepts from information geometry allow us to locally approximate the mutual information. By utilizing this approximation the main privacy-utility trade-off problem can be rewritten as a quadratic optimization problem that has closed-form solution under some constraints. For the cases where the closed-form solution is not obtained we provide lower bounds on it. In contrast to the previous works that have complexity issues, here, we provide simple privacy designs with low complexity which are based on finding the maximum singular value and singular vector of a matrix. To do so, we follow two approaches where in the first one we find a lower bound on the main problem and then approximate it, however, in the second approach we approximate the main problem directly.
   In this work, we present geometrical studies of the proposed methods and in a numerical example we compare our results considering both approaches with the optimal solution and the previous methods. Finally, we discuss how the proposed methods can be applied to deal with differential privacy.
C1 [Zamani, Amirreza; Skoglund, Mikael] KTH Royal Inst Technol, Div Informat Sci & Engn, Stockholm, Sweden.

TI Zero Estimation Cost Strategy for Witsenhausen Counterexample with
   Causal Encoder
SO 2025 IEEE INTERNATIONAL SYMPOSIUM ON INFORMATION THEORY, ISIT
SE IEEE International Symposium on Information Theory
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 International Symposium on Information Theory-ISIT-Annual
CY JUN 22-27, 2025
CL Ann Arbor, MI
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc, IEEE Information Theory Society
ID INFORMATION
AB We propose a zero estimation cost (ZEC) scheme for causal-encoding noncausal-decoding vector-valued Witsenhausen counterexample based on the coordination coding result. In contrast to source coding, our goal is to communicate a controlled system state. The introduced ZEC scheme is a joint control-communication approach that transforms the system state into a sequence that can be efficiently communicated using block coding. The noncausal decoder receives sufficient information for reconstructing the system state perfectly, enabling the achievable estimation cost to be zero. Numerical results show that our approach significantly reduces the power budget required for achieving zero-estimation-cost state reconstruction at the decoder. In the second part, we introduce a more general non-zero estimation cost (Non-ZEC) scheme. We observe numerically that the Non-ZEC scheme operates as a time-sharing mechanism between Witsenhausen's original two-point strategy and the ZEC scheme. Overall, by leveraging block-coding gain, our proposed methods substantially improve the power-estimation trade-off for Witsenhausen counterexample.

CL Luxembourg, LUXEMBOURG
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc, University of Luxembourg
DE Electric Bus; Dynamic Charging; SUMO; Vehicle-to-Vehicle charging;
   Mobile charging stations
ID NETWORK
AB Recent advances in battery technologies and a global push for greener transport have accelerated the development of electrified public transportation systems. Such systems often face challenges due to the need for large battery capacities and the high costs associated with conventional charging infrastructure. This study examines the potential of Mobile Autonomous Charging Pods (MAPs), which are autonomous charging vehicles, as an innovative solution to enhance both the efficiency and cost-effectiveness of electric bus operations in urban environments. Using the case of inner-city trunk bus lines in Stockholm and employing a microscopic simulation-based study, three charging scenarios are evaluated: depot charging only, depot combined with end-station charging, and depot plus MAP charging. The results indicate that the integration of MAPs can significantly reduce the required battery capacities and associated infrastructure costs while enhancing the reliability of the service. By facilitating dynamic, on-the-go charging, MAPs offer a sustainable and economically viable alternative for urban electric bus networks.

TI A staged biologically mediated process for phosphorus recovery from
   marine sediments via enrichment of polyphosphate-accumulating organisms
   and struvite precipitation
SO JOURNAL OF ENVIRONMENTAL CHEMICAL ENGINEERING
LA English
DT Article
DE Phosphorus recovery; Marine sediment; Biological concentration; PAO
   enrichment; Struvite precipitation; PHREEQC modelling
ID REMOVAL; PAO
AB Phosphorus (P) is a critical but finite resource, while its internal release from sediments accelerates eutrophication. Developing recovery strategies that target sediments could simultaneously support resource recycling and environmental remediation. Here, we propose a biologically mediated two-stage P recovery strategy using marine sediments, where Stage I enriches polyphosphate-accumulating organisms (PAOs), and Stage II concentrates and recovers P via struvite precipitation. In Stage I, results demonstrated that PAOs can be enriched in natural marine sediments through cyclic aerobic-anaerobic cultivation. After 49 days, PAO abundance in sediment increased from 0.06% to 7.1% at the genus level, with the sediment-inoculated sequencing batch reactor (SBR) showing strong P release and uptake rates of 8.61 and 8.43 mg P/g VSS & centerdot;h(-1), respectively. In Stage II, extended anaerobic operation enhanced PAO selection and metabolic capacity, as shown by increased polymer synthesis rates (poly-P: 4.84 mg P/g VSS & centerdot;h(-1); glycogen: 14.92 mg C/g VSS & centerdot;h(-1)) and enrichment of carbon metabolism genes. PAO abundance surged to 41.2% within 10 days and ultimately reached 52.1% in the sediment reactor, while remaining below 7% in the sludge reactor. Supernatant P concentrations reached 99.5 mg/L in the sediment system, enabling effective struvite precipitation. PHREEQC simulations and laboratory precipitation confirmed recovery efficiencies > 95%. These findings demonstrate that biologically activated sediments can serve as a functional platform for concentrating low-P sediment-derived streams through PAO-mediated P cycling, followed by downstream struvite recovery.

TI Comparative Study of DRIE-Based Fabrication Strategies for
   Silicon-Micromachined Subterahertz Waveguide Components
SO IEEE TRANSACTIONS ON COMPONENTS PACKAGING AND MANUFACTURING TECHNOLOGY
LA English
DT Article
DE Etching; Fabrication; Silicon; Surfaces; Switches; Technology;
   Microelectromechanical systems; Surface roughness; Metallization;
   Couplers; Deep reactive-ion etching (DRIE); microelectromechanical
   system (MEMS); silicon micromachining; subterahertz (THz) switching;
   waveguide components
ID BANDPASS-FILTERS; TECHNOLOGY
AB Deep reactive-ion etching (DRIE) is widely used to realize high-aspect-ratio silicon waveguide components for terahertz (THz) and sub-THz applications. However, fabrication-induced variations in the waveguide cavity surface quality and geometry can significantly influence the conductor loss and device performance. At sub-THz frequencies, the electromagnetic (EM) skin depth becomes comparable to the characteristic scale of the surface roughness, while geometrical deviations can alter the EM behavior. In this work, two distinct DRIE-based fabrication strategies-a direct-etching approach and a release-assisted etching approach-are implemented to isolate the fabrication-induced surface morphology from the geometric integrity in silicon-micromachined waveguide cavities, providing insight into DRIE-based micromachining process optimization. Detailed geometrical and surface characterizations using scanning electron microscopy (SEM) and an optical profilometer reveal that the release-assisted etching approach effectively reduces the waveguide sidewall underetch from a maximum of 10.4-2.8 & micro;m while introducing increased sidewall surface roughness from 41.4 to 107.0 nm. A 163-183-GHz silicon-micromachined microelectromechanical system (MEMS) waveguide crossover switch is used as the test vehicle for RF characterization. The measurements show that, despite the reduction of the sidewall underetch, the measured isolation remains at a similar level for the two strategies. On the contrary, the increased sidewall roughness is accompanied by an approximately 33% increase in the crossover-state insertion loss (IL). These results indicate that, for the investigated device, surface roughness has a stronger impact on RF performance than sidewall underetch. The findings provide practical fabrication-level guidelines for DRIE process optimization and for the future development of increasingly complex high-frequency silicon-micromachined waveguide devices and microsystems.
C1 [Yu, Suxian; Tian, Xu; Shah, Umer; Oberhammer, Joachim] KTH Royal Inst Technol, Dept Micro & Nanosyst, S-10044 Stockholm, Sweden.

TI User Mobility Demands Near-Field Communications in Terahertz-Band
   Wireless Networks Beyond 6G
SO IEEE TRANSACTIONS ON COMMUNICATIONS
LA English
DT Article
DE Bandwidth; Arrays; Distance measurement; Apertures; Modeling; Joining
   processes; Limiting; Joints; Signal to noise ratio; Gain; Terahertz
   communications; millimeter wave; near field; spherical wave propagation;
   directional antennas
ID MIMO COMMUNICATIONS; CHALLENGES; CHANNELS; ANTENNA
AB Near-field propagation is often unavoidable at terahertz (THz) frequencies due to the large apertures needed for sufficient array gain, yet near-field operation complicates practical system design, especially under user mobility. This paper asks whether a mobile THz link can remain broadband and achieve the desired high rates and coverage while operating exclusively in the radiative far field. To answer this question, we develop a proof-by-contradiction feasibility framework that jointly enforces 1) a far-field requirement based on the Fraunhofer distance and 2) a reliability requirement specified by a target SNR at the worst-case link distance. We derive closed-form upper bounds on the far-field-feasible bandwidth for stationary and mobile links. We further incorporate practical misalignment through representative UE-rotation and mobility scenarios. Numerical results show that stationary THz links can remain far-field-only with physically realizable apertures while supporting extremely large bandwidths, whereas guaranteeing such operation for practical mobile THz systems becomes highly restrictive. In practically relevant mobile THz access settings, the far-field-feasible bandwidth becomes a severe limiting factor: achieving bandwidths of tens of gigahertz would require unrealistically high UE transmit power. A cross-band comparison further shows that far-field-only operation is largely attainable at sub-6 GHz and, to a significant extent, at mmWave for moderate bandwidths, while near-field effects should be accounted for in the design of mobile THz systems.

TI Safe Multi-Agent Deep Reinforcement Learning-Based Energy Management for
   Multi-Microgrid Energy Communities
SO IEEE TRANSACTIONS ON CONSUMER ELECTRONICS
LA English
DT Article
DE Feeds; Radio broadcasting; Frequency modulation; Oscillators; Circuits;
   Feedback; Circuits and systems; Internet of Things; Communication
   systems; Internet; Multi-microgrid; energy management system; safe deep
   reinforcement learning; decentralized; deep neural network
ID ARCHITECTURE
AB Multi-microgrid (MMG) systems enhance efficient integration of renewables and emerging loads into modern sustainable communities. By facilitating energy sharing among MGs, MMG systems can enhance reliability, improve efficiency, and promote economic operation. In this paper, a projection based safe multi-agent deep reinforcement learning (DRL) algorithm is developed for the decentralized energy management of multi-microgrid energy communities. The safe projection layer integrated into DRL training effectively restricts the agent's actions to the feasible space, thereby preventing any violations of physical constraints during system operation. The decentralized application of the proposed safe DRL methods achieves the optimal energy management without high-fidelity communication, thereby facilitating practical deployment. The modified MMG test systems are adopted to test the proposed method, and the testing results could verify the effectiveness of the proposed energy management system in achieving cost-effective operation while guaranteeing 100% safety and eliminating the dependence on high-fidelity communication.

TI Enhanced visible-light photo-fenton-like degradation of microplastics
   using nitrogen-doped TiO2and cobalt Prussian blue analogue
SO JOURNAL OF ENVIRONMENTAL CHEMICAL ENGINEERING
LA English
DT Article
DE Microplastic degradation; Polypropylene microplastics; Photo-Fenton-like
   process; Immobilized photocatalyst; Visible-light photocatalyst;
   Continuous-flow reactor
ID TIO2
AB Microplastic pollution is a global concern infiltrating every aspect of our environment and food chain. Conventional wastewater treatment plants (WWTPs) are ineffective at removing microplastics (MPs), necessitating advanced degradation approaches. Among various techniques, Fenton and photo-Fenton processes emerge as promising solutions. Here, photo-Fenton-like degradation was studied using nitrogen-doped titania (N-TiO2) nanorods decorated with cobalt Prussian blue analogue (N-TiO2/CoPBA) as a visible-light-active photocatalyst. The N-TiO(2)nanorods were hydrothermally grown on glass fiber, followed by CoPBA deposition through co-precipitation. The glass fiber serves the dual function of trapping plastic particles and supporting the photocatalyst. FTIR was used to monitor the carbonyl index, indicating progressive oxidation. The observed changes are consistent with oxidative degradation and polymer-chain scission; however, the formation of soluble degradation intermediates and the extent of mineralization were not evaluated in the present study. Under visible-light irradiation in a flow-through reactor, photocatalytic and photo-Fenton-like degradation of polypropylene (PP) MPS were compared, with photo-Fenton-like reactions showing substantial particle volume reduction. Optimizing H(2)O(2)concentration (15 mM, dosing every 24 h) led to a similar to 90% volume reduction within 48 h while it took several days when H(2)O(2)was not controlled. Post-experiment analysis of the catalyst confirmed its stability, highlighting its potential for long-term application in microplastic removal from water streams.

TI Sustainability in engineering education: an editorial and interpretive
   synthesis
SO EUROPEAN JOURNAL OF ENGINEERING EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Sustainability in engineering education; engineering education;
   education for sustainable development; integration; transformation;
   decolonisation
ID DECOLONIZATION; TRANSFORMATION
AB This editorial provides an introduction to and interpretive synthesis of the seventeen contributions to the European Journal of Engineering Education special issue on Sustainability in Engineering Education, and the current state, emerging trends, and future directions of the field. The special issue contributions indicate that sustainability in engineering education is moving beyond early add-on approaches towards broader curricular integration, yet transformative ambitions remain only partially realised and unevenly enacted across institutional, disciplinary, and national contexts. These insights are complemented by two additional perspectives: a reflective insider account of current trends at one European university and a critical challenge to dominant European viewpoints through South African scholarship on transformation and decolonisation. Beyond further curricular integration, the next phase of sustainability in engineering education is argued to depend on enhanced institutional reflexivity, epistemic plurality, and critical engagement with the purposes, assumptions, and societal responsibilities of engineering education and engineers.

TI Charge-cloud-based micrometer resolution in deep-silicon photon-counting
   CT
SO JOURNAL OF MEDICAL IMAGING
LA English
DT Article
DE computed tomography; deep-silicon detectors; edge-on sensors;
   photon-counting detectors; spatial resolution
ID PHASE-RETRIEVAL; RAY
AB Purpose We are developing a monolithic deep-silicon photon-counting sensor targeting spatial resolution on the order of 1 mu m. This work investigates how pixel pitch, noise level, threshold number, and threshold placement affect the achievable tangential and wafer-thickness resolution to guide the CMOS electronics and sensor design. Approach Allpix Squared simulations were used to evaluate pixel pitches with two noise levels and two threshold-placement schemes over varying threshold numbers. Interaction position was estimated in both dimensions using lookup tables trained on the thresholded pixel output, and performance was quantified using the MTF. Results Decreasing pixel pitch strongly improved tangential resolution for Compton interactions, whereas photoelectric interactions showed a weaker dependence on pixel pitch and threshold placement. Equal-counting thresholds approached the achievable resolution with fewer thresholds than equidistant thresholds. For eight equal-counting thresholds, the 10% MTF across the studied pixel sizes ranged from 1284 lp/cm to 2452 lp/cm in the tangential direction and from 15 to 71 lp/cm in the wafer-thickness direction for Compton interactions, compared with 409 to 507 lp/cm and 18 to 79 lp/cm, respectively, for photoelectric interactions. RMSE analysis further showed that some interactions for the 25 mu m pitch reached below 2 mu m tangentially and a few tens of micrometers in the wafer-thickness direction. Conclusions Spatial resolution improved with a threshold number up to about 8 to 10, beyond which additional programmable thresholds gave only modest gain. The achieved tangential resolution also suggests that analyzer-free phase-contrast imaging is feasible for realistic geometries and expected interference patterns.

TI An exact system optimum model for the schedule-based transit assignment
   problem
SO JOURNAL OF PUBLIC TRANSPORTATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Schedule-based transit assignment problem; Exact system optimum; Hard
   capacity; Multi-line network; Spatial-temporal competing demand flows
ID OPTIMAL TRAFFIC ASSIGNMENT; PEAK-HOUR CONGESTION; PUBLIC TRANSPORT;
   STRATEGIES; OPERATION; EFFICIENT; NETWORKS; TIME
AB Mass transit systems in many cities are facing growing congestion. One widely studied approach to alleviating this congestion involves solving the schedule-based transit assignment problem (STAP). STAP employs a joint choice model of departure times and routes, defining a spatial-temporal path wherein passengers decide both when to depart and which route to take. User equilibrium (UE) models for the STAP indicate the current congestion cost, while system optimum (SO) models can provide insights for congestion relief directions. However, most existing STAP studies under the SO condition rely on approximate SO (Approx. SO) models, which underestimate the potential for congestion reduction in the system. The few studies in STAP that compute exact SO solutions ignore realistic constraints such as hard capacity, multi-line networks, and spatial-temporal competing demand flows. This paper proposes an exact SO method for the STAP that overcomes these limitations. We apply our approach to a case study involving part of the Hong Kong Mass Transit Railway network, which includes 5 lines, 12 interacting origin-destination (OD) pairs and 52,717 passengers. Computing an Approx. SO solution for this system indicates a modest potential for congestion reduction measures, with a cost reduction of 17.39% from the UE solution. Our exact SO solution is 36.35% lower than the UE solution, which is more than double the potential for congestion reduction. We then show how the exact SO solution can be used to identify: (i) which OD pairs have the most potential to reduce congestion; (ii) how many passengers can be reasonably shifted; (iii) future systems' potential with increasing demand and expanding network capacity.

TI "Same Voice, Different Language": An Exploration of Voice-Cloned
   Translation to Support Non-Native Speakers in Online Meetings
SO PROCEEDINGS OF THE 2026 INTERNATIONAL CONFERENCE ON INTELLIGENT USER
   INTERFACES, IUI 2026
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 31st International Conference on Intelligent User Interfaces-IUI
CY MAR 23-26, 2026
CL Paphos, CYPRUS
SP Association for Computing Machinery
DE Voice Cloning; Cross-Lingual Communication; Intelligent User Interfaces;
   Mental Workload; User Experience
AB Cross-lingual meetings have become essential for global collaboration, yet current translation technologies often strip away vocal identity the unique speaker characteristics that convey nuance and social presence. While generic text-to-speech (TTS) provides basic intelligibility, it creates a disconnect between speakers and their translated voices, potentially undermining engagement and comprehension. This paper investigates whether voice cloning technology can bridge this gap by preserving speaker identity in real-time translation. We present a controlled study comparing four voice conditions in meeting interpretation: original speech, gender-neutral TTS, gender-matched TTS, and voice cloning. Through a within-subjects experiment with 45 participants, we demonstrate that voice cloning significantly reduces mental workload (p <.001) and enhances user experience across pragmatic quality (p <.001), hedonic quality (p <.001), and overall satisfaction (p <.001) compared to traditional TTS. While original speech maintained advantages in naturalness, voice cloning achieved superior intelligibility, social impression, and user preference. Qualitative analysis revealed that participants valued voice cloning for preserving speaker identity and improving conversation tracking in multi-speaker scenarios. Our findings suggest that identity-preserving translation represents a significant advancement for cross -lingual communication systems, offering both cognitive and social benefits. We conclude with design implications for integrating voice cloning into meeting platforms while addressing ethical considerations around consent and transparency.

TI Modeling non-linear temperature dependence of thermal expansion
   coefficient in Ni-based superalloys
SO JOURNAL OF MATERIALS RESEARCH AND TECHNOLOGY-JMR&T
LA English
DT Article
DE Thermal expansion; CALPHAD-based modeling; Ni-based superalloys; Alloy
   design; Heat treatment; Haynes 282
ID THERMOPHYSICAL PROPERTIES; ALLOYING ELEMENTS; NICKEL; CR; AL;
   MICROSTRUCTURE; MOLYBDENUM; RESISTANCE; ALUMINUM; BEHAVIOR
AB The compatibility in coefficients of thermal expansion (CTE) between components is crucial in designing materials that can withstand large temperature variations without cracking or failing. For commercial Ni-based superalloys, which are important for aerospace applications, experimental measurements have shown that CTEs can exhibit a non-linear temperature dependence near the solvus temperature of gamma '(L12) precipitates. Existing models in the literature often fail to describe this non-linear dependence, as they typically assume linearity or apply only to specific model alloys, ignoring the nuances of composition and heat treatment. In this work, we present a multi-phase CTE model calibrated using experimental data, from which the effect of chemical composition on CTE of each phase is determined. Effect of heat treatment is considered by calculating the phase fraction at treatment temperature. The model-predicted CTE agrees well with experimental measurements for commercial Ni-based superalloys. Based on the model predictions, suggestions for developing new composition and heat treatments to tune the CTE of Ni-based superalloys are proposed.

TI The complexity of disciplinary literacy and identity in STEM: A case
   study of student language use in project-based learning
SO LINGUISTICS AND EDUCATION
LA English
DT Article
DE Disciplinary literacy; Project-based learning; STEM education; Identity;
   Multilingual university
ID ENGLISH-MEDIUM INSTRUCTION; HIGHER-EDUCATION; LECTURERS; DISCOURSE
AB Disciplinary literacy has gained more recognition in STEM education as understanding subject is inextricably linked to mastering specialised ways of communicating science to different audiences. In projectbased learning (PBL), STEM students must apply such literacies across multiple communicative contexts. However, current pedagogies often prioritise high-stakes academic communication while overlooking the everyday interactional processes through which student disciplinary literacy and identities are developed. This study explores the communicative practices of a group of engineering students during a PBL course in Sweden. Using a linguistic ethnography and case study approach, the study followed the group's project trajectory, drawing on self-reflection journals, student documents, observations and interviews to examine how these students navigated disciplinary literacy in learning. A mid-course academic failure, a "crisis moment," served as an analytical entry point to trace shifts in the students' multilingual and multi-sited communication and identity construction. The findings reveal the students' communication and identity struggles across academic, workplace, and interpersonal domains were closely interconnected, and that students constructed a "vulnerable student" identity through multilingual and multi-sited communicative practices to navigate institutional uncertainty and access support. The study highlights disciplinary literacy as an interactional, identity-mediated practice and emphasizes the need for STEM education to move beyond text-centric approach to support students' identity work and communicative competence across contexts. TI Guide Policy-Enabled Safe Reinforcement Learning for Coordinated
   Frequency Control in Low-Inertia Grids With Inverter Interface Resources
SO IEEE TRANSACTIONS ON INDUSTRIAL INFORMATICS
LA English
DT Article; Early Access
DE Modeling; Frequency; Training; Frequency control; Learning (artificial
   intelligence); Filtering; Filters; Inverters; Safety; Timing;
   grid-forming inverters; guide policy; multiagent reinforcement; planning
ID STRATEGY
AB Frequency stability in inverter-based power systems is increasingly challenged due to the reduction of mechanical inertia and damping. Although several learning-based frequency control methods have been developed for low-inertia grids, they still exhibit limitations in action safety, learning efficiency, and policy coordination. This article proposes a guide policy-enabled multiagent reinforcement learning method for coordinated and safe frequency control. Specifically, a guide policy-based action filter corrects improper actions and forms a safe curriculum for the training policy. Moreover, a surrogate model-enabled planning process promotes safe exploration and policy coordination by refining potentially unsafe actions through temporary simulated experiences, without applying those actions directly to the environment. In addition, a behavior-cloning mechanism mitigates environmental nonstationarity and stabilizes multiagent learning. Electromagnetic transient simulations on a modified IEEE 39-bus system show that the proposed method reduces the maximum frequency deviation of VSG5 from -0.2556 to -0.0709 Hz and its maximum rate of change of frequency from -4.9268 to -0.7575 Hz/s while improving action safety, learning efficiency, and policy coordination over existing methods.

TI Predictive Beamforming With Distributed MIMO
SO 2025 8TH INTERNATIONAL BALKAN CONFERENCE ON COMMUNICATIONS AND
   NETWORKING, BALKANCOM
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 8th International Balkan Conference on Communications and
   Networking-BALKANCOM-Annual-Empowering Connections, Enabling Innovation
CY JUN 17-20, 2025
CL Piraeus, GREECE
SP IEEE Indonesia Section, IEEE Greece Section, University of Piraeus, Technology Innovation Institute
DE Sensing-assisted beamforming; predictive beamforming; extended Kalman
   filter; road-side unit; distributed MIMO
AB In vehicle-to-everything (V2X) applications, roadside units (RSUs) can be tasked with both sensing and communication functions to enable sensing-assisted communications. Recent studies have demonstrated that distance, angle, and velocity information obtained through sensing can be leveraged to reduce the overhead associated with communication beam tracking. In this work, we extend this concept to scenarios involving multiple distributed RSUs and distributed MIMO (multiple input, multiple output) systems. We derive the state evolution model, formulate the extended Kalman-filter equations, and implement predictive beamforming for distributed MIMO. Simulation results indicate that, when compared with a colocated massive MIMO antenna, distributed antennas lead to more uniform and robust sensing performance, coverage, and data rates, while the vehicular user is in motion.

TI A randomized field experiment comparing nudges and boosts for household
   electricity conservation in student housing
SO SCIENTIFIC REPORTS
LA English
DT Article
ID SOCIAL NORMS; BEHAVIOR
AB Behavioral interventions can promote household energy conservation, yet evidence on the mediators and moderators driving their effects remains limited. In a 24-week randomized field experiment with 140 student households, we contrasted two behavioral interventions: a social comparison "nudge" with a competence-enhancing "boost". Specifically, the nudged group received weekly normative feedback on their electricity use relative to their peers, while the boosted participants accessed device-specific energy-saving tips. Both treatments significantly reduced electricity consumption relative to control by attenuating the seasonal increase observed among untreated households, corresponding to avoided consumption of similar to 7 MWh. Exploratory analyses indicated that the effects were concentrated among participants with higher baseline consumption. Within this group, the Boost intervention was associated with a statistically significant reduction of 3.97 kWh per week relative to control. The nudged participants reduced their weekly consumption by 3.12 kWh. While both interventions performed similarly over time, evidence for competence enhancement was limited: competence scores increased modestly in the boost group relative to control, but this effect became marginal after correcting for multiple testing. Further evidence indicates that intervention effects were strongest among participants with higher pre-treatment competence and environmental motivation, suggesting a moderating role of these variables.

TI Viscoelasticity of biomimetic scale beams from trapped complex fluids
SO BIOINSPIRATION & BIOMIMETICS
LA English
DT Article
DE nonlinear viscoelasticity; biological slime; complex fluids; nonlinear
   elasticity; biomimetics
ID FILM RUPTURE; DESIGN; MUCUS; HYDRODYNAMICS; EVOLUTION; SURFACES; LAYER;
   FLOW
AB We investigate the nonlinear viscoelastic behavior of a biomimetic scale-covered beam in which shear-dependent complex fluids are trapped between overlapping scales under bending loads. These fluids mimic biological mucus and slime layers commonly enveloping the skins found in nature. An energy-based analytical model is developed to quantify the interplay between substrate elasticity, scale geometry, and fluid rheology at multiple length scales. Constant strain rate and oscillatory bending are examined for Newtonian, shear-thinning, and shear-thickening fluids. The analysis reveals unique, geometry- and rate-dependent viscoelastic response, distinct from classical mechanisms such as material dissipation, frictional resistance, or air drag. Energy dissipation is shown to emerge from a nonlinear coupling of tribological parameters, fluid rheology, and system kinematics, exhibiting distinct regime-differentiated characteristics. The model captures the competitions and cooperations between elastic and geometrical parameters to influence the viscoelastic behavior and lead to geometry and rheology scaling laws for relative energy dissipation. The pronounced nonlinearity in the moment-curvature relationships, along with the geometry-controlled regimes of performance, highlights the potential for using tailored and engineered complex inks for soft robotics and smart damping systems.

TI Communication-Efficient Stochastic Distributed Learning
SO IEEE TRANSACTIONS ON AUTOMATIC CONTROL
LA English
DT Article
DE Training; Convergence; Costs; Distance learning; Computer aided
   instruction; Vectors; Stochastic processes; Accuracy; Federated
   learning; Technological innovation; Distributed learning; local
   training; stochastic optimization; variance reduction
ID OPTIMIZATION; CONVERGENCE; CONSENSUS; ADMM
AB We address distributed learning problems, both nonconvex and convex, over undirected networks. In particular, we design a novel algorithm based on the distributed to tackle the challenges of high communication costs, and large datasets. Our design deals with these challenges: first, by enabling the agents to perform multiple local training steps between each round of communications; second, by allowing the agents to employ stochastic gradients while carrying out local computations. We show that the proposed algorithm converges to a neighborhood of a stationary point, for nonconvex problems, and of an optimal point, for convex problems. We also propose a variant of the algorithm to incorporate variance reduction, and thus achieving exact convergence. We show that the resulting algorithm indeed converges to a stationary (or optimal) point, and moreover that local training accelerates convergence. We thoroughly compare the proposed algorithms with the state of the art, both theoretically and through numerical results.

TI Multi-terminal Strong Coordination over Noisy Channels with Encoder
   Cooperation
SO 2025 IEEE INTERNATIONAL SYMPOSIUM ON INFORMATION THEORY, ISIT
SE IEEE International Symposium on Information Theory
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 International Symposium on Information Theory-ISIT-Annual
CY JUN 22-27, 2025
CL Ann Arbor, MI
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc, IEEE Information Theory Society
ID MULTIPLE-ACCESS CHANNEL
AB We investigate the problem of strong coordination over a multiple-access channel (MAC) with cribbing encoders. In this configuration, two encoders observe independent and identically distributed (i.i.d.) samples of a source random variable each and encode the inputs to the MAC. The decoder which observes the output of the MAC together with side-information, must generate approximately i.i.d. samples of another random variable which is jointly distributed with the two sources and the side information. We also allow for possible encoder cooperation, where one of the encoders can non-causally crib from the other encoder's input. Independent pairwise shared randomness is assumed between each encoder and the decoder at limited rates. Firstly, in the presence of cribbing, we derive an achievable region based on joint source-channel coding. We also prove that in the absence of cribbing, our inner bound is tight for the special case when the MAC is composed of deterministic links, and the sources are conditionally independent given the side information. We then explicitly compute the regions for an example both with and without cribbing between the encoders, and demonstrate that cribbing strictly improves upon the achievable region.

TI Exploring the urban symbiosis potential of vertical farming systems: the
   case of Stockholm city
SO CLEANER AND RESPONSIBLE CONSUMPTION
LA English
DT Article
DE Vertical farming; Resource efficiency; Industrial symbiosis; Urban
   symbiosis; Circular economy
ID INDUSTRIAL SYMBIOSIS; GREENHOUSE; HORTICULTURE; KNOWLEDGE; NETWORKS;
   IMPACTS
AB An increasing urban population is leading to a growing demand for food, putting pressure on already overexploited resources. In regions with limited agricultural land and short growing seasons, such as Sweden, dependence on food imports makes the food systems particularly vulnerable to geopolitical instability and supply chain disruptions. Vertical farming systems (VFSs) have been proposed as a strategy to increase food selfsufficiency and resilience in urban areas. However, they are characterized by high energy consumption and greenhouse gas emissions. Urban symbiosis (UrS), the exchange of resource flows among organizations within the urban area, could improve VFSs' resource use efficiency and environmental performance. Yet, empirical evidence on UrS implementation in VFSs remains limited. This study investigates the resource flows of commercial VFSs in the City of Stockholm, assesses the current status of UrS, identifies potential synergies, and examines the key barriers and drivers influencing its implementation. Semi-structured interviews were conducted with representatives of five VFSs, complemented by on-site visits and secondary data analysis. The results show that all studied VFSs rely on similar key inputs, most of which are sourced from outside Sweden. Moreover, despite respondents' familiarity with potential symbiotic opportunities, UrS implementation remains limited. Four themes were identified as influencing adoption: resource compatibility and availability, business strategy, infrastructure, and economic feasibility. Output-based exchanges, such as waste heat recovery and reuse of organic waste, were found more feasible than input-based exchanges. The study suggests that facilitated UrS networks, supported by municipalities and academic partners, may represent the most suitable development model.

TI One-Bit Consensus of Controllable Linear Multiagent Systems With
   Communication Noises
SO IEEE TRANSACTIONS ON AUTOMATIC CONTROL
LA English
DT Article
DE Radio broadcasting; Frequency modulation; Oscillators; Feedback
   circuits; Output feedback; Protocols; Communication systems;
   Communication networks; Modulation; Communication noise; consensus;
   controllable linear multiagent systems (MASs); Markovian switching
   topologies; one-bit data rate
ID COORDINATION
AB This article addresses the one-bit consensus of controllable linear multiagent systems (MASs) with communication noises. A consensus algorithm consisting of a communication protocol and a consensus controller is designed. The communication protocol introduces a linear compression encoding function to achieve a one-bit data rate, thereby saving communication costs. The consensus controller with a stabilization term and a consensus term is proposed to ensure the consensus of a potentially unstable but controllable MAS. Specifically, in the consensus term, we adopt an estimation method to overcome the information loss caused by one-bit communications and a decay step to attenuate the effect of communication noise. Two combined Lyapunov functions are constructed to overcome the difficulty arising from the coupling of the control and estimation. By establishing similar iterative structures of these two functions, this article shows that the MAS can achieve consensus in the mean square sense at the rate of the reciprocal of the iteration number under the case with a connected fixed topology. Moreover, the theoretical results are generalized to the case with jointly connected Markovian switching topologies by establishing a certain equivalence relationship between the Markovian switching topologies and a fixed topology. Two simulation examples are given to validate the algorithm.

TI A Study of Multimodal Pen plus Gaze Interaction Techniques for Shape
   Point Translation in Extended Reality
SO 2025 IEEE INTERNATIONAL SYMPOSIUM ON MIXED AND AUGMENTED REALITY, ISMAR
SE International Symposium on Mixed and Augmented Reality
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 International Symposium on Mixed and Augmented Reality-ISMAR-Annual
CY OCT 08-12, 2025
CL Daejeon, SOUTH KOREA
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc
DE Input techniques; vr; eye-tracking; gaze interaction
ID INPUT
AB Eye-tracking offers new ways to augment our interaction possibilities in extended reality. This paper investigates how gaze can assist pen users in translating shape points within graphical models. By leveraging gaze, we can support the usual design activities with an option where objects can be selected and repositioned through eye movements, with the pen serving as a confirmation tool. This can reduce manual effort and enhance efficiency and ergonomics. To evaluate its effectiveness, we compare four interaction techniques: two pen-based baselines (direct and ray-based) and two gaze-supported methods (gaze for selection and/or object dragging), using a probability based selection scheme. In a user study, 16 participants carried out a shape point translation task and their performance, effort, and user experience were measured. The results highlight the performance trade-offs of each technique-while the gaze-based dragging method introduced marginally more errors, it significantly reduced task time. Our findings offer comparative insights into the strength and limitations of gaze-and pen-based interaction methods, supporting the design of future multimodal 3D design tools.

TI A Dynamic Hydrogen-Bonded Organic Framework With Giant OFF-ON
   Birefringence
SO ANGEWANDTE CHEMIE-INTERNATIONAL EDITION
LA English
DT Article; Early Access
DE birefringence porous organic frameworks; crystal transformation; guest
   inclusion; high-performance optical devices; optical properties
ID POLYCARBONATE; TRANSITION
AB Birefringence, the optical property enabling materials to split light based on polarization and propagation direction, is crucial for many technological fields, spanning from telecommunications to imaging and photonics. Particularly, controlling the birefringence is vital in high-performance applications such as nonlinear optics, adaptable spectroscopy filters, adaptive imaging, for biomedical, and space applications. Most known switchable birefringent materials suffer from either low birefringence values or narrow tuneable values, thus hindering their use in dynamic optical systems. Here, a novel approach based on a dynamic hydrogen-bonded organic framework (HOF) capable of reversible single crystal to single crystal transformation, during guest molecule inclusion is reported. Changes in the planar conformation of the pi-pi stacking between layers during guest inclusion drastically alters the birefringence off-on, from negligible values to 0.73, surpassing all known switchable materials. This approach represents a novel platform for the development of high-performance optical devices.

TI Wideband mmWave antenna with integrated 1 x 8 feed-array configuration
   for high-gain 5G wireless applications using characteristic mode theory
SO SCIENTIFIC REPORTS
LA English
DT Article
DE 28 GHz; 38 GHz; CMT; High gain; Integrated feed network array; Linear
   array antennas
AB This work presents a wideband millimeter-wave (mmWave) antenna designed and experimentally validated using the Characteristic Mode Theory (CMT). The proposed single-element is implemented on an ultra-thin 0.254 mm substrate and occupies a compact footprint of 8 & times; 6 mm2. The antenna achieves a wide impedance bandwidth from 22.5 to 39 GHz, corresponding to a fractional bandwidth of 53.66%, covering the major 28 GHz and 38 GHz 5G mmWave bands. CMT analysis is employed to investigate the dominant electric-dipole modes across various frequency bands. Further, by keeping the ground length constant at , the monople structure is varied to suppress the magnetic-dipole modes, thereby improving the linear polarization behavior with electric-dipole modes. The CMT for the proposed single-element structure illustrates that modes , , , and are contributing to resonance at 24, 28, 32, and 36 GHz, where the first modes in each band indicate the dominant mode with relatively higher magnitude. The summation of these multiple excited modes across multiple bands has led to a wideband resonance with a stable radiation pattern. To improve the radiation performance for high-gain mmWave applications, the optimized single element is extended into a 1 & times; 8 linear array configuration. The proposed array achieves a gain variation from 11 to 15.8 dBi across the operating band, with peak gains of 12.8 dBi at 28 GHz and 15.5 dBi at 38 GHz. The measured results obtained from the developed prototype show good agreement with the simulated responses, validating the proposed CMT-guided design approach. Due to its compact size, ultra-thin profile, wide bandwidth, high-gain array performance, and clear modal interpretation, the proposed antenna is a promising candidate for future 5G mmWave wireless communication systems.

TI The fitness landscape of a form II rubisco in a photosynthetic bacterium
   guides engineering of oxygen tolerance
SO SCIENCE ADVANCES
LA English
DT Article
ID CARBON ASSIMILATION; PROTEIN; CATALYSIS; CYANOBACTERIA; SPECIFICITY;
   STABILITY; SELECTION; ALIGNMENT; GROWTH; ALGAE
AB Ribulose-1,5-bisphosphate carboxylase/oxygenase (rubisco) is an important but challenging protein engineering target. Fast and selective rubiscos could enhance photosynthesis in plants and accelerate biobased production processes. To facilitate engineering of rubisco, we applied an in vivo screen that couples rubisco activity to growth rate of the photoautotrophic cyanobacterium Synechocystis sp. PCC 6803. We screened a barcoded mutagenesis library of the form II rubisco from Gallionella sp. containing 15,000 single-site and multi-site variants. Exchanges in loop 6 near the active site, at the dimer interface, and in potential gas tunnels improved rubisco fitness. The dataset also informed protein engineering, using recombination and a trained transformer model. In vitro characterisation of two high-fitness variants showed reduced catalytic efficiency for oxygenation (kcat/Ko) in both and an increased carboxylation turnover (kcatC) in one. This large labeled fitness dataset, containing examples of epistasis, can be useful for benchmarking computational models of rubisco.

TI Cross-Reality for Autonomous Mobility in Autistic Individuals:
   Evaluating At-Home VR Training and In-Situ AR Support in a Field Study
SO 2025 IEEE INTERNATIONAL SYMPOSIUM ON MIXED AND AUGMENTED REALITY, ISMAR
SE International Symposium on Mixed and Augmented Reality
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 International Symposium on Mixed and Augmented Reality-ISMAR-Annual
CY OCT 08-12, 2025
CL Daejeon, SOUTH KOREA
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc
DE Accessible technologies; Interactive learning environments; Cross
   reality; Virtual reality; Augmented reality; Conversational agents
ID AUGMENTED REALITY; SPECTRUM DISORDER; ADOLESCENTS; CHILDREN; STUDENTS
AB Autonomy is a central concern for many caregivers of autistic individuals, particularly in domains such as mobility, where environmental complexity and variability pose significant challenges. In this work, we explore a cross-reality approach to support the development of autonomous public transportation skills-specifically intercity train travel-through a combination of Virtual Reality (VR), in-situ Augmented Reality (AR), and a conversational agent. Our system provides at-home VR training to simulate travel scenarios, AR-based support during real-world travel, and interactive guidance through a conversational interface, creating a cohesive and adaptive learning experience. We conducted a field study with ten participants over three months, which showed autonomy improvements comparable to state-of-the-art methods. Our findings contribute updated insights and design considerations for the application of cross-reality systems in accessibility and mobility training for neurodiverse users.

TI Fluid Antenna Port Prediction based on Large Language Models
SO GLOBECOM 2025-2025 IEEE GLOBAL COMMUNICATIONS CONFERENCE
SE IEEE Global Telecommunications Conference (Globecom)
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 Global Communications Conference-GLOBECOM
CY DEC 08-12, 2025
CL Taipei, TAIWAN
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc
DE Fluid antennas; large language models; channel prediction; moving port
   prediction; Port-LLM
AB This study seeks to utilize large language models (LLMs) to forecast the moving ports of fluid antenna (FA). By repositioning the antenna to the locations identified by our proposed model, we intend to address the mobility challenges faced by user equipment (UE). To the best of our knowledge, this paper introduces, for the first time, the application of LLMs in the prediction of FA ports, presenting a novel model termed Port-LLM. The architecture of our model is based on the pre-trained GPT-2 framework. We designed specialized data preprocessing, input embedding, and output projection modules to effectively bridge the disparities between the wireless communication data and the data format utilized by the pre-trained LLM. Simulation results demonstrate that our model exhibits superior predictive performance under different numbers of base station (BS) antennas and varying UE speeds, indicating strong generalization and robustness ability. Furthermore, the spectral efficiency (SE) attained by our model surpasses that achieved by traditional methods in both medium and high-speed mobile environments.

TI A SDR System for Passive UAV Detection with Deep Learning Method
SO GLOBECOM 2025-2025 IEEE GLOBAL COMMUNICATIONS CONFERENCE
SE IEEE Global Telecommunications Conference (Globecom)
LA English
DT Proceedings Paper
CT 2025 Global Communications Conference-GLOBECOM
CY DEC 08-12, 2025
CL Taipei, TAIWAN
SP Institute of Electrical and Electronics Engineers Inc
DE wireless sensing; deep learning; unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV); channel
   state information (CSI); software defined radio (SDR)
AB Unmanned aerial vehicles (UAVs) have emerged as an important tool for communication research in recent years. However, they introduce new challenges for modern urban management. Conventional UAV detection methods, which rely on either vision-based systems or dedicated sensor networks, incur significant deployment complexity and high maintenance costs. To address these challenges, this paper proposes a communication signal-based UAV detection system. Given the poor signal quality passively scattered by the UAV, we employ signal processing techniques to enhance the feature extraction, while implementing customized modifications to the model architecture to accommodate the characteristics of complex-valued inputs. To validate our approach, we conducted comprehensive tests using a software-defined radio transceiver system constructed by USRP-2974 devices. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed method outperforms other methods and achieves a detection accuracy surpassing 99% in real-world environments.
